I was alone in my apartment with the windows closed.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: When I was buzzing with ideas I’d skip meals without noticing, and now food tastes like cardboard, so my rehearsal clothes have been hanging looser than usual.\n- key: S027\n  name: Sleep_Disturbance\n  description: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.\n  manifestation: My sleep has been all over the place—some nights I stayed up sketching formations until dawn, and lately I fall asleep exhausted but wake at four or five with my stomach tight.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: Recently even crossing the studio to pick up my notebook feels slowed down, like my body is moving through wet sand instead of music.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: I feel wiped out after small things, like answering one email to a dancer or showering with this ankle brace on, and lying down doesn’t really recharge me.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep replaying the cancelled performances and telling myself I let the dancers, the funders, and the whole community down, even though people have been kinder about it than I deserve.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: There were stretches when I felt dazzlingly alive and then suddenly furious if anyone questioned the plan or reminded me my ankle still needed rest.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: Even with the injury, I couldn’t stop moving from task to task—drawing maps, emailing collaborators, reorganizing music files, and trying to redesign the whole project in one rush.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I honestly believed I could turn this setback into a career-defining work that would change how people think about dance and wilderness, and I was shocked when others didn’t immediately see it.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: My collaborators told me I was talking so fast in meetings that they couldn’t get a word in, and I remember feeling like if I paused the whole idea would disappear.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’d sit down to finish a grant report and suddenly the sound of a truck outside, a photo on the wall, or one phrase in an old rehearsal note would pull me into a completely different plan.\n- key: S040\n  name: Increased_Goal_Activity\n  description: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.\n  manifestation: I started contacting presenters, dancers I barely knew, and old professors with long proposals, as if I had to build an entire new network for the piece right that minute.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: the wired stretch when I was barely sleeping and racing through plans. I’m still showing up for essentials, but I’m not functioning like I normally do as a choreographer or teacher.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This whole pattern started after my ankle injury and has been going on for about two months now.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The depressed part has been present for about nine months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 34 / dancer_or_choreographer / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S024\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been able to experience pleasure when you're doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been paying close enough attention to say yes or no. Sometimes if I’m doing something, it’s not bad, but I can’t tell if it feels like pleasure the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that you've done and actually felt happy about?\n\nPatient: I’m having a hard time thinking of something specific. Maybe small moments felt okay, but I don’t know if I’d call it happy exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in activities that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve noticed I don’t get the same pull toward things, but I can’t clearly say the pleasure is gone. I mean, I don’t reach for things the way I used to, but I haven’t really paid attention to whether that’s loss of interest or just feeling low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been able to experience pleasure when you're doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been paying close enough attention to say yes or no. Sometimes if I’m doing something, it’s not bad, but I can’t tell if it feels like pleasure the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that you've done and actually felt happy about?\n\nPatient: I’m having a hard time thinking of something specific. Maybe small moments felt okay, but I don’t know if I’d call it happy exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in activities that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve noticed I don’t get the same pull toward things, but I can’t clearly say the pleasure is gone. It’s more like... things feel kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I’ve been getting wiped out by really small things, like answering one email to a dancer or even just showering with this ankle brace on. And lying down doesn’t really seem to recharge me much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a bit. I haven’t been weighing myself much, but my rehearsal clothes feel looser lately. When I was really buzzing with ideas I’d just skip meals without realizing, and now food doesn’t taste like much… kind of like cardboard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: Sluggish, more than restless. Lately even just crossing the studio to pick up my notebook feels slow, like my body is moving through wet sand instead of moving with the music.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I keep going over the cancelled performances in my head and thinking I let everyone down—the dancers, the funders, people in the community. Everyone’s been pretty kind about it, honestly, but I keep feeling like I don’t really deserve that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt really low and ashamed, but I haven’t been thinking about hurting myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, not as depressed?\n\nPatient: Not really. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty sadness. It feels like the lights are on in the studio, but there’s no music in me. I haven’t had much of a break from that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven’t had much of a break from that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It’s like I’m there, the day is starting, but there’s no music in me anymore. That’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About nine months, I think. It’s been there most of that time—the depressed feeling, especially in the mornings.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Some things still feel okay once I’m doing them, but I haven’t been paying close attention to whether I enjoy them the same way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t reach for things the way I used to, but I haven’t really paid attention to whether that’s loss of interest or just feeling low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been able to experience pleasure when you're doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been paying close enough attention to say yes or no. Sometimes if I’m doing something, it’s not bad, but I can’t tell if it feels like pleasure the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that you've done and actually felt happy about?\n\nPatient: I’m having a hard time thinking of something specific. Maybe small moments felt okay, but I don’t know if I’d call it happy exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in activities that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve noticed I don’t get the same pull toward things, but I can’t clearly say the pleasure is gone. It’s more like... things feel kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I’ve been getting wiped out by really small things, like answering one email to a dancer or even just showering with this ankle brace on. And lying down doesn’t really seem to recharge me much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a bit. I haven’t been weighing myself much, but my rehearsal clothes feel looser lately. When I was really buzzing with ideas I’d just skip meals without realizing, and now food doesn’t taste like much… kind of like cardboard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: Sluggish, more than restless. Lately even just crossing the studio to pick up my notebook feels slow, like my body is moving through wet sand instead of moving with the music.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I keep going over the cancelled performances in my head and thinking I let everyone down—the dancers, the funders, people in the community. Everyone’s been pretty kind about it, honestly, but I keep feeling like I don’t really deserve that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt really low and ashamed, but I haven’t been thinking about hurting myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, not as depressed?\n\nPatient: Not really. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty sadness. It feels like the lights are on in the studio, but there’s no music in me. I haven’t had much of a break from that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. I’ll sit down to do something simple, like finish a grant report, and then some little thing pulls me off track—a truck going by outside, a photo on the wall, even one phrase in old rehearsal notes. Suddenly I’m thinking about a completely different plan, and I haven’t made any progress on what I meant to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to, or is it more that you just don't feel like doing things?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I feel slowed down. Like even crossing the studio to pick up my notebook takes effort. My body feels like it’s moving through wet sand instead of just... moving normally.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt nearly normal, without any depression?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. It’s been pretty constant—waking up with that heavy, hollow sadness. Even when I’m in the studio, it feels like the lights are on but there’s no music in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep thinking I’ve become a burden, or at least that I’ve let everyone down. I replay the cancelled performances over and over and tell myself I failed the dancers, the funders, the community… even though people have been kinder to me than I feel like I deserve.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or moving more quickly than you used to, or is it just that you don't feel like doing things?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I’m moving slower, not quicker. Like I said, even just walking across the studio to get my notebook feels like a lot. My body feels heavy, like I’m moving through wet sand.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
